Page78 
included. The practical component may involve problem-solving related to radiobiological 
effects and basic dosimetric calculations or simulations. 
 
RMM603: Ultrasound & Doppler I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This introductory course covers the fundamental principles of 
diagnostic medical sonography (ultrasound) and Doppler imaging. The course includes 
ultrasound physics, instrumentation, image formation, artifacts, scanning techniques, and 
basic applications in imaging abdominal organs, obstetrics, and gynecology. Principles of 
Doppler ultrasound for vascular assessment are also introduced. 
 
RMM604: Women Imaging 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the specialized imaging modalities and 
techniques used for the diagnosis and management of conditions specific to women's health. 
The course covers mammography, breast ultrasound, breast MRI, obstetric ultrasound, 
gynecological ultrasound, and imaging of the female pelvis. Screening guidelines, 
diagnostic protocols, and interpretation of common findings are discussed.  
 
RMM605: Contrast Media Examinations 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a comprehensive understanding of the types, 
properties, administration, and safety considerations of contrast media used in various 
radiological examinations. The course covers iodinated contrast media, gadolinium-based 
contrast agents, barium sulfate suspensions, and ultrasound contrast agents. Pharmacology, 
indications, contraindications, patient screening, management of adverse reactions, and 
imaging protocols for contrast-enhanced studies (e.g., angiography, urography, GI studies, 
enhanced CT/MRI) are detailed. The practical component focuses on preparation, safe 
administration techniques, patient monitoring, and recognizing and managing contrast 
reactions in simulated or clinical settings.

 تكنولوجيا العلوم كلية
الصحية التطبيقية 
 جامعة الريادة للعلوم

Page79 
 
RMM606: Positron Emission Tomography (PET) 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles and applications of Positron 
Emission Tomography (PET) imaging, often integrated with CT (PET/CT). The course 
covers the physics of positron emission, PET radiopharmaceuticals (especially FDG), 
scanner instrumentation, image acquisition and reconstruction, data analysis, common 
clinical applications (oncology, neurology, cardiology), and radiation safety aspects of PET. 
The practical component involves observation of PET/CT procedures, familiarization with 
image processing, and understanding quality control measures specific to PET imaging. 
 
RMM701: Ultrasound & Doppler II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: RMM603 Ultrasound & Doppler 
I 
• 
Course Description: This advanced course builds upon Ultrasound & Doppler I, covering 
specialized applications and advanced techniques in diagnostic medical sonography. The 
course explores musculoskeletal ultrasound, pediatric sonography, small parts (thyroid, 
scrotum), advanced vascular Doppler applications, and an introduction to interventional 
ultrasound. Image optimization, advanced artifact recognition, and interpretation of 
complex cases are emphasized. 
 
RMM702: Radiobiology & Dosimetry II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: RMM602 Radiobiology & 
Dosimetry I 
• 
Course Description: This course is a continuation of Radiobiology & Dosimetry I, delving 
deeper into the applications of radiobiological principles in radiotherapy and advanced 
dosimetry. The course covers tumor radiobiology, normal tissue tolerance, radiation-
induced carcinogenesis, modern radiotherapy fractionation schedules, and advanced 
dosimetric concepts and calculations for external beam therapy and brachytherapy. Quality 
assurance in dosimetry and treatment delivery is a significant focus.

Page80 
 
RMM703: MRI Techniques II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: RMM403 MRI Physics  
• 
Course Description: This advanced course focuses on specialized Magnetic Resonance 
Imaging (MRI) techniques and their clinical applications. The course covers advanced pulse 
sequences, MR angiography, diffusion-weighted imaging, perfusion imaging, functional 
MRI, cardiac MRI, and musculoskeletal MRI protocols. Protocol optimization, artifact 
troubleshooting, image post-processing, and patient management for specialized MRI 
examinations are key areas.  
 
RMM704: Nuclear Medicine I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This introductory course covers the fundamental principles and 
techniques of nuclear medicine imaging. The course includes basic nuclear physics, 
radiopharmaceuticals, instrumentation (gamma cameras, SPECT), image acquisition and 
processing, quality control, radiation safety, and common clinical applications such as bone 
scans, thyroid scans, renal scans, and lung scans. The practical component involves 
familiarization with nuclear medicine equipment, observation of procedures, understanding 
quality control tests, and basics of radiopharmaceutical handling and administration under 
supervision. 
 
RMM705: Interventional & Cardiovascular Radiology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles and techniques of interventional 
radiology and specialized cardiovascular imaging. The course covers an overview of 
common vascular and non-vascular interventional procedures (e.g., angiography, 
angioplasty, stenting, biopsies, drainages), cardiac catheterization, and advanced 
cardiovascular imaging techniques. Patient assessment, procedural guidance, equipment, 
contrast media, and post-procedure care are discussed.

Page81 
RMM801: Bone Densitometry Techniques 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the principles, techniques, and clinical 
applications of bone densitometry, primarily Dual-Energy X-ray Absorptiometry (DXA). 
The course covers the pathophysiology of osteoporosis and other metabolic bone diseases, 
principles of DXA technology, scan acquisition protocols for spine, hip, and forearm, 
quality control procedures, interpretation of results (T-scores, Z-scores), and radiation 
safety.  
 
RMM802: Nuclear Medicine II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: RMM704 Nuclear Medicine I 
• 
Course Description: This advanced course builds on Nuclear Medicine I, covering more 
specialized procedures, advanced imaging techniques, and therapeutic applications. The 
course includes SPECT/CT, advanced cardiac nuclear medicine, neurological applications, 
infection/inflammation imaging, radionuclide therapies (e.g., I-131 for thyroid cancer), and 
current developments in nuclear medicine. In-depth understanding of image analysis and 
quantification is emphasized.  
 
RMM803: Basics of Radiopathology II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: RMM504 Basics of 
Radiopathology I 
• 
Course Description: This course continues the study of pathological conditions and their 
radiological manifestations, building upon the foundations of Radiopathology I. The course 
delves into the imaging appearances of specific diseases within various organ systems (e.g., 
cardiovascular, 
neurological, 
gastrointestinal, 
genitourinary), 
integrating 
pathophysiological mechanisms with imaging findings. Emphasis is placed on differential 
diagnosis and the role of imaging in disease staging and follow-up.

RMM805: Research Project 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (6 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This capstone course requires students to undertake an independent 
research project or a comprehensive literature review on a topic relevant to Technology of 
Radiology and Medical Imaging sciences, under the supervision of a faculty member. The 
course includes guidance on research design, literature searching and critique, data 
collection and analysis methods (if applicable), ethical considerations in research, scientific 
writing, and presentation skills. The practical component involves conducting research or 
review, regular consultations with the supervisor, and data interpretation. The course 
culminates in a written research report/dissertation and an oral presentation, demonstrating 
the student's ability to apply scientific inquiry and communicate findings effectively. 
 
Elective Requirements for Technology of Radiology and Medical Imaging 
(RME) 
 
RME01: Quality Control in Radiology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the principles and procedures of quality 
control (QC) and quality assurance (QA) in a radiology department to ensure optimal image 
quality and patient safety. The course covers QC tests for various imaging equipment (X-
ray, CT, MRI, Ultrasound, Mammography), establishment of QA programs, performance 
monitoring, acceptance testing, regulatory requirements, and documentation. The impact of 
QC/QA on diagnostic accuracy and operational efficiency is emphasized. 
 
RME02: PACS & RIS in Radiology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a comprehensive overview of Picture Archiving 
and Communication Systems (PACS), and Radiology Information Systems (RIS) used in 
modern radiology practice. The course covers the architecture and components of 
PACS/RIS, image acquisition and storage, display workstations, workflow management,

RME03: Dental Radiology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course is designed to provide student with theoretical knowledge 
and practical experience to understand the dental anatomy, radiological modalities, and the 
different dental examination techniques. 
 
RME04: Radiology Emergency 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the role of medical imaging in emergency and 
trauma settings. The course covers imaging protocols and interpretation of common 
findings in various emergency situations, including acute neurological events, chest 
emergencies, abdominal emergencies, and polytrauma. Emphasis is placed on rapid and 
accurate image acquisition, communication with the emergency team, and understanding 
the specific challenges of imaging critically ill or injured patients. 
 
RME05: Nanotechnology in Medicine 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) 
• 
Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the emerging field of nanotechnology and its 
potential applications in medicine, with a particular focus on diagnostic imaging and 
therapy. The course covers the fundamentals of nanomaterials, their unique properties, and 
their use as contrast agents, drug delivery vehicles, and in theranostics. Current research, 
potential benefits, challenges, and ethical considerations of nanomedicine in radiology and 
beyond will be explored.

Course Description of Technology of Biomedical Equipment 
Program (ME)  
 
Mandatory Requirements for Biomedical Equipment Technology (MEM) 
 
MEM301: Electrical Circuits 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a fundamental understanding of electrical circuit 
theory. The course covers DC and AC circuit analysis, including Ohm's law, Kirchhoff's 
laws, network theorems (Thévenin, Norton, Superposition), analysis of RLC circuits, 
impedance, reactance, phasors, power, and resonance. The practical component involves 
hands-on laboratory experiments where students build and test various DC and AC circuits, 
use measurement instruments (multimeters, oscilloscopes), and verify theoretical concepts. 
 
MEM402: Instruments & Electrical Measurements 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the principles and operation of various 
electrical and electronic instruments used for measurement and testing. The course covers 
measurement standards, error analysis, principles of operation of analog and digital 
multimeters, oscilloscopes, signal generators, power supplies, and an introduction to 
transducers and data acquisition systems.  
 
MEM303: Hospital Design and Safety 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the principles of hospital design, layout, and safety 
regulations critical for healthcare environments. The course addresses the functional design 
of different hospital departments, workflow optimization, space planning, utility 
requirements (electrical, medical gases), and infection control considerations in design. A 


--- page 91 ---
 تكنولوجيا العلوم كلية
الصحية التطبيقية 
 جامعة الريادة للعلوم
والتكنولوجي
ا 
 
EL Sadat City El Mehwer El Markazy 2 
Cairo Alexandria Desert RD K92 
info@rst.edu.eg

 مدينة السادات-
 
 المحور المركزي2
 
 طريق القاهرة
 االسكندرية الصحراوي الكيلو92
 
 
Page90 
significant portion is dedicated to safety standards, including electrical safety, fire safety, 
radiation safety, chemical safety, and biological hazard management within hospitals. 
 
MEM401: Biomechanics 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles of mechanics as applied to 
biological systems. The course covers the study of forces and motion in the human body, 
mechanical properties of biological tissues (bones, muscles, ligaments, tendons), and the 
analysis of human movement. Topics include statics, dynamics, kinematics, and kinetics of 
human motion. 
 
MEM302: Computer Programming 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a foundation in computer programming for 
biomedical applications. The course introduces fundamental programming concepts, 
including data types, control structures (loops, conditionals), functions, arrays, and an 
introduction to object-oriented programming concepts using a relevant high-level language 
(C/C++). The practical component consists of hands-on programming exercises and 
projects aimed at solving problems relevant to biomedical engineering, such as data 
analysis, signal processing basics, and simple algorithm implementation. 
 
MEM403: Electronics 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the fundamentals of electronic devices and 
circuits. The course covers semiconductor physics, diodes, bipolar junction transistors 
(BJTs), field-effect transistors (FETs), operational amplifiers (op-amps), and their 
characteristics and applications in rectification, amplification, filtering, and signal 
conditioning. 
 
MEM404: Electrical Machines & Power Electronics

Course Description: The course includes the study of transformers, DC motors and 
generators, AC motors (induction and synchronous), and stepper motors. The power 
electronics section introduces power semiconductor devices (diodes, thyristors, power 
transistors) and their applications in rectifiers, inverters, and DC-DC converters, relevant 
for medical equipment power supplies. 
 
MEM501: Advanced Programming 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: MEM402 Computer Programming 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on advanced programming techniques and their 
application in biomedical engineering. The course cover topics such as data structures 
(linked lists, stacks, queues, trees), algorithm design and analysis, object-oriented 
programming in depth, and interfacing with hardware or data acquisition systems using a 
relevant programming language. 
 
MEM502: Clinical Engineering 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the management of medical technology in 
healthcare settings. The course covers the role of clinical engineers, medical equipment 
lifecycle management (acquisition, acceptance testing, maintenance, decommissioning), 
safety standards and regulations for medical devices, risk management, incident 
investigation, and quality assurance programs for medical equipment. The practical 
component may involve case studies, development of maintenance schedules, simulated 
acceptance testing, and understanding of hospital equipment management systems. 
 
MEM503: Applied Mathematics 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers mathematical concepts and techniques essential 
for solving problems in biomedical engineering. The course typically includes topics such 
as differential equations (ordinary and partial), linear algebra (vectors, matrices,

MEM504: Digital Circuits 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the fundamentals of digital logic circuits. The 
course includes number systems, Boolean algebra, logic gates, combinational logic circuit 
design and analysis (adders, multiplexers, decoders), sequential logic circuits (flip-flops, 
counters, registers), and an introduction to programmable logic devices and memory. 
 
MEM601: Digital Signal Processing 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the fundamentals of digital signal processing 
(DSP) with an emphasis on biomedical signals. The course covers discrete-time signals and 
systems, sampling theorem, Z-transform, Discrete Fourier Transform (DFT), Fast Fourier 
Transform (FFT), design of digital filters (FIR and IIR), and applications of DSP in noise 
removal, feature extraction, and analysis of physiological signals like ECG, EEG, and EMG. 
 
MEM602: Bioinformatics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: AHM303 Bioinformatics I  
• 
Course Description: Building directly on the foundations established in Bioinformatics I, 
this advanced course explores sophisticated computational techniques for analyzing 
complex biological data. Topics include multiple sequence alignment, gene prediction, 
Next-Generation Sequencing (NGS) data analysis, comparative genomics, transcriptomics, 
proteomics, and an introduction to systems biology. The practical component focuses on 
applying advanced bioinformatics software and pipelines to analyze diverse biological

MEM603: Bioelectronics I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the application of electronic principles to 
biological and medical systems, particularly in the context of biosignal acquisition and 
conditioning. The course covers the origin of biopotentials (ECG, EEG, EMG), 
characteristics of biomedical signals, design of bioamplifiers, filters (active and passive) for 
noise reduction, electrical safety in medical devices, and basics of electrodes and 
transducers used for biomedical measurements. The practical component involves 
designing, building, and testing bioelectronic circuits for amplifying and filtering simulated 
or actual biopotentials. 
 
MEM604: Embedded Systems and IoT 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides an in-depth introduction to the architecture, 
programming, and application of microcontrollers in biomedical devices, with a focus on 
integrating Embedded Systems and the Internet of Things (IoT). Students will explore 
microcontroller architecture (CPU, memory, I/O ports, timers, ADC) and learn 
programming in both assembly and C languages. The course also covers interrupting 
handling, interfacing with sensors, actuators, and display devices, with an emphasis on 
biomedical applications. Additionally, students will learn how to design and implement IoT-
based solutions by integrating embedded systems with sensors and actuators for real-time 
data collection and control in healthcare, automation, and smart home contexts. Practical 
components involve hands-on laboratory work using platforms like Arduino or PIC to 
design and program biomedical systems, control external devices, and acquire data for 
various medical applications

MEM605: Therapeutic & Prosthetic Equipment I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles and operation of various 
therapeutic and prosthetic devices used in healthcare. The course covers devices such as 
infusion pumps, dialysis machines, ventilators, electrosurgical units, defibrillators, 
pacemakers, and basic prosthetic limbs and assistive devices. Emphasis is placed on their 
working principles, safety features, operational checks, and common troubleshooting. 
 
MEM606: Clinical Laboratory Instrumentation 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) 
• 
Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the technology, principles of operation, and 
maintenance of instrumentation used in clinical laboratories. The course covers automated 
analyzers for hematology, clinical chemistry, and immunoassay, as well as specialized 
instruments like spectrophotometers, chromatographs, and cell counters. The course 
emphasizes understanding the analytical principles, calibration, quality control, and 
troubleshooting of these instruments from a biomedical technology perspective. 
 
MEM701: Bioelectronics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: MEM603 Bioelectronics I 
• 
Course Description: This advanced course builds upon Bioelectronics I, delving into more 
complex bioelectronic systems and interfaces. The course covers advanced topics in 
biosensor technology, neural interfaces, brain-computer interfaces (BCI), implantable 
electronic devices, biotelemetry, and advanced signal processing techniques for noise 
cancellation and feature extraction from biopotentials. Electrical safety standards for 
medical devices are also discussed in depth.  
 
MEM702: Therapeutic and Prosthetic Equipment II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: MEM605 Therapeutic & 
Prosthetic Equipment I

performance evaluation, and regulatory aspects are explored. 
 
MEM703: Biomedical Equipment I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a comprehensive overview of a wide range of 
diagnostic biomedical equipment commonly found in hospitals. The course covers the 
principles of operation, functional blocks, applications, and basic maintenance of imaging 
equipment (e.g., X-ray, ultrasound basics), patient monitoring systems (ECG, blood 
pressure, SpO2), and basic physiological measurement devices. 
 
MEM704: Biomedical Instrumentation and Physiological Signals 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course integrates the study of physiological signals with the 
instrumentation used to acquire and analyze them. The course covers the origin, 
characteristics, and physiological significance of various biosignals (ECG, EEG, EMG, 
EOG, respiratory signals, blood pressure). It also delves into the design and application of 
instrumentation systems for sensing, amplifying, filtering, processing, and displaying these 
signals. 
 
MEM705: Medical Biomaterials 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC)  
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the properties, selection, and application of 
biomaterials used in medical devices and tissue engineering. The course covers the 
classification of biomaterials (metals, polymers, ceramics, composites), their mechanical 
and chemical properties, biocompatibility, host-tissue interactions, material degradation,

MEM801: Cybersecurity in Medical Devices 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None  
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the critical area of cybersecurity in medical 
devices, exploring the challenges and solutions for protecting medical technologies from 
cyber threats. Students will gain an understanding of the unique risks that medical devices 
face, including data breaches, hacking, and vulnerabilities that could compromise patient 
safety and privacy. The course covers industry standards, regulations (such as FDA and 
HIPAA), and best practices for securing medical devices. Key topics include risk 
management, encryption, authentication, and secure communication protocols. Through 
case studies and practical exercises, students will learn how to design, implement, and 
maintain secure medical devices and systems in a rapidly evolving technological landscape. 
 
MEM802: Fault Detection and Preventive Maintenance 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers strategies and techniques for detecting faults in 
medical equipment and implementing effective preventive maintenance programs. The 
course addresses common failure modes of medical devices, diagnostic tools and techniques 
for troubleshooting, principles of reliability engineering, development of preventive 
maintenance schedules, calibration procedures, and documentation practices. The 
importance of safety and regulatory compliance in maintenance activities is emphasized. 
The practical component involves hands-on troubleshooting exercises on various medical 
devices (or simulators), performing preventive maintenance tasks, and using test equipment.

MEM803: Biomedical Equipment II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: MEM703 Biomedical Equipment 
I 
• 
Course Description: This course is a continuation of Biomedical Equipment I, providing a 
more in-depth study of advanced diagnostic and therapeutic medical equipment. The course 
potentially includes advanced imaging modalities (CT, MRI), life support systems 
(ventilators, anesthesia machines in depth), and specialized therapeutic devices. System 
integration, networking, and advanced troubleshooting are key aspects. 
 
MEM804: Graduation Project 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (0 LEC, 6 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This capstone experience requires students to apply their accumulated 
knowledge and skills to a significant biomedical equipment technology project. This could 
involve the design and development of a medical device prototype, a comprehensive study 
and improvement of existing medical technology, or an in-depth research project related to 
biomedical instrumentation or healthcare technology management. The involves 
independent and group work, research, design, development, testing, and regular 
consultations with a faculty supervisor. The course culminates in a comprehensive written 
report and a formal presentation of the project and its outcomes. 
 
Elective Requirements for Biomedical Equipment Technology (MEE) 
 
MEE01: Healthcare Quality Systems and Standards 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles of quality management systems 
and relevant standards in the healthcare technology sector. The course covers concepts of 
quality assurance, quality control, continuous quality improvement (CQI) methodologies, 
ISO standards (e.g., ISO 9001, ISO 13485 for medical devices), accreditation processes, 
risk management, and regulatory requirements related to the quality of medical equipment 
and healthcare services.

MEE02: Biomedical Optics 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course explores the principles of optics and their application in 
biomedical instrumentation and diagnostics. The course covers the fundamentals of light, 
lasers, optical fibers, light-tissue interaction, and the principles behind various biomedical 
optical techniques such as spectroscopy, microscopy, endoscopy, pulse oximetry, and 
optical coherence tomography (OCT). Applications in medical imaging, diagnostics, and 
therapeutics are discussed. 
 
MEE03: Biomedical Nanotechnology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the emerging field of biomedical 
nanotechnology, focusing on its applications in diagnostics, therapeutics, and medical 
devices. The course covers nanoscale materials (nanoparticles, nanotubes, quantum dots), 
their unique properties, fabrication methods, and applications in areas such as targeted drug 
delivery, biosensors, medical imaging contrast agents, and tissue engineering. Ethical and 
safety considerations of nanotechnology in medicine are also discussed. 
 
MEE04: Healthcare Technology Management 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the strategic and operational aspects of 
managing medical technology within healthcare organizations. The course covers topics 
such as technology assessment, acquisition planning, equipment inventory management, 
maintenance strategies, service contracts, staff training, asset tracking, and financial 
management related to healthcare technology. The role of technology in improving 
healthcare delivery and patient safety is emphasized. 
 
MEE05: Mathematical Modeling and Applications 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (1 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None

Course Description: This course focuses on the development and application of

The course introduces various modeling techniques, which may include deterministic and 
stochastic models, compartmental models, and models based on differential equations or 
statistical methods. Applications in areas such as pharmacokinetics, physiological system 
modeling, epidemiology, or biomechanics are explored. The practical component involves 
developing and simulating mathematical models using software tools (e.g., MATLAB, 
Simulink) and interpreting the results in a biomedical context. 
 
MEE06: Data Design & Analysis 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (2 LEC, 2 TUT/LAB) - Prerequisites: None  
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the principles of experimental design, data 
acquisition, and statistical analysis relevant to healthcare. The course covers topics such as 
research methodology, types of data, data collection techniques, database design basics, 
descriptive statistics, probability distributions, hypothesis testing, ANOVA, regression, and 
correlation. The practical component involves using statistical software packages (R, or 
Python libraries) to analyze biomedical datasets, design experiments, interpret statistical 
results and Data Visualization.

Course Description for Technology of Nutrition and Food Safety 
Program (NS) 
 
Mandatory Requirements for Technology of Nutrition and Food Safety (NSM) 
 
NSM301 Medical Parasitology 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course aims to provide students with fundamental knowledge 
about medically important parasites and their impact on human health, with an emphasis on 
those related to nutrition and food safety. It covers parasite classification, life cycles, modes 
of transmission, pathogenic mechanisms, and clinical symptoms of common parasitic 
diseases. It also addresses laboratory diagnosis, treatment, and prevention of parasitic 
infections, focusing on foodborne and waterborne parasites. 
 
NSM302 Biochemistry of Macro and Micronutrients 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the chemical structure, digestion, absorption, 
metabolism, and biological functions of macronutrients (carbohydrates, lipids, proteins) and 
micronutrients (vitamins, minerals) in the human body. The course addresses major 
metabolic pathways, the role of enzymes and hormones in regulating these pathways, and 
biochemical interactions related to human health and disease. It also covers nutrient balance 
and the importance of each nutrient in maintaining normal bodily functions.  
 
NSM303 Basic Nutrition 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the fundamental principles of nutrition science 
and their application in maintaining individual and community health. It covers basic 
concepts related to different nutrients, their functions, food sources, and recommended daily 
allowances. The course also discusses the foundations of balanced dietary planning, the

 تكنولوجيا العلوم كلية

Page106 
importance of energy and energy balance, and the role of proper nutrition in health 
promotion and disease prevention. 
 
NSM401 Nutritional Assessment 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course aims to equip students with the knowledge and skills 
necessary to assess the nutritional status of individuals and groups. The course covers 
various methods of nutritional assessment, including Anthropometric measurements, 
Biochemical assessment, Clinical assessment, and Dietary assessment (ABCD). The course 
also addresses the interpretation of nutritional assessment data and the identification of 
malnutrition and nutritional risks. 
 
NSM402 Nutrition in the Life Cycle 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course examines the specific nutritional needs and challenges 
across various stages of the human life cycle, from preconception, pregnancy, lactation, 
infancy, childhood, adolescence, adulthood, to old age. It focuses on the physiological and 
nutritional changes occurring at each stage and the impact of nutrition on growth, 
development, and overall health. The course discusses common nutritional problems in each 
age group and how to design dietary recommendations and interventions tailored to meet 
specific needs and improve health and nutritional status throughout the different life stages. 
 
NSM403 Meal and Diet Planning 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course aims to provide students with the practical knowledge 
and skills required to plan balanced and healthy meals and diets for individuals and diverse 
groups. The course covers principles of meal planning, national and international dietary 
guidelines, calculation of energy and nutrient requirements, and designing menus that meet 
the needs of specific populations (e.g., athletes, vegetarians, individuals with specific health 
conditions).

Page107 
 
NSM404 Advanced Nutritional Biochemistry- Integrated Metabolism 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course delves deeper into the intricate biochemical pathways of 
nutrient metabolism and their integration and regulation within the human body. Theoretical 
lectures will cover advanced topics in the metabolism of carbohydrates, lipids, and proteins, 
including their interrelationships, hormonal control, and adaptation to various physiological 
states (e.g., fasting, exercise, stress). Emphasis will be placed on the molecular mechanisms 
underlying nutrient utilization, storage, and the consequences of metabolic dysregulation. 
 
NSM501 Food Microbiology 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces students to the fundamentals of microbiology 
with a specific focus on microorganisms relevant to food. The theoretical part covers the 
types of microorganisms (bacteria, yeasts, molds, viruses, protozoa) found in food, their 
growth characteristics, and factors affecting their survival and proliferation. It explores the 
role of microorganisms in food spoilage, foodborne illnesses, and food fermentation. 
Principles of food preservation and control of microbial growth will be discussed.  
 
NSM502 Therapeutic Nutrition I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: The course covers etiology, pathophysiology, and metabolic 
alterations of common health conditions, and the role of nutrition in their prevention and 
management. Topics include MNT for overweight and obesity, diabetes mellitus, 
cardiovascular diseases (e.g., hypertension, dyslipidemia), and selected gastrointestinal 
disorders. Students will learn to apply the nutrition care process, including assessment, 
diagnosis, intervention, and monitoring/evaluation for these conditions. 
 
 
 


--- page 109 ---

Page108 
NSM503 Food Analysis 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides a comprehensive understanding of the principles 
and techniques used in the chemical and physical analysis of food components. The course 
cover methods for sampling, sample preparation, and analysis of macronutrients (proteins, 
fats, carbohydrates, fiber), micronutrients (vitamins, minerals), moisture, ash, and other 
food constituents like additives and contaminants. The course will also discuss quality 
assurance, good laboratory practices, and the interpretation of analytical results in the 
context of food quality, safety, and labeling regulations. 
 
NSM504 Sports Nutrition 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course explores the application of nutritional principles to 
enhance athletic performance and recovery. The course covers energy metabolism during 
exercise, the role of macronutrients and micronutrients in sports, fluid and electrolyte 
balance, and the use of ergogenic aids. It addresses the specific nutritional needs for 
different types of sports (endurance, strength/power, team sports) and various athletic 
populations. Students will learn how to assess the nutritional status of athletes and develop 
evidence-based dietary strategies to optimize training, competition, and overall health. 
 
NSM601 Principles of Food & Water Safety 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 1, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course cover common foodborne and waterborne hazards 
(microbial, chemical, physical), factors contributing to contamination, and principles of 
hazard control throughout the food chain, from production to consumption. It will introduce 
food safety management systems like HACCP (Hazard Analysis and Critical Control 
Points), Good Hygienic Practices (GHP), and Good Manufacturing Practices (GMP). Water 
quality standards, treatment methods, and prevention of waterborne diseases will also be 
discussed.

Page109 
NSM602 Community Nutrition and Intervention 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers methods for community nutritional status 
assessment, identification of vulnerable groups, and common public health nutrition 
problems (e.g., micronutrient deficiencies, chronic diseases). The course explores the 
development, implementation, and evaluation of community-based nutrition programs and 
interventions aimed at promoting health and preventing disease. Topics include nutrition 
policy, health promotion strategies, culturally appropriate nutrition education, and the role 
of intersectoral collaboration in improving community nutrition outcomes. 
 
NSM603 Malnutrition 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides an in-depth examination of the spectrum of 
malnutrition, encompassing both undernutrition (e.g., protein-energy malnutrition, 
micronutrient deficiencies) and overnutrition (overweight and obesity). The course covers 
etiology, pathophysiology, clinical manifestations, metabolic consequences, and public 
health significance of various forms of malnutrition across the lifespan. It also addresses 
diagnostic criteria, assessment methods, and evidence-based strategies for prevention and 
management at individual and community levels. 
 
NSM604 Food Service Management 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the principles and practices of managing food 
service operations in various settings, such as hospitals, schools, and commercial 
establishments. These course topics include menu planning and development, food 
purchasing and procurement, inventory control, food production systems, quality 
management, food safety and sanitation in food service, equipment selection and layout, 
financial management, and human resource management specific to the food service 
industry.

Page110 
NSM605 Infection Control for Nutritionists 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the chain of infection, standard and transmission-
based precautions, principles of asepsis, disinfection, sterilization, and management of 
infectious waste. It will address the role of nutritionists in preventing healthcare-associated 
infections (HAIs), foodborne illness outbreaks in institutional settings, and providing safe 
nutritional support to immunocompromised individuals.  
 
NSM606 Therapeutic Nutrition II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: NSM502 Therapeutic Nutrition I 
• 
Course Description: This course is a continuation of Therapeutic Nutrition I, expanding 
on the application of medical nutrition therapy (MNT) for more complex disease states. The 
course will cover the nutritional management of renal diseases, liver diseases, cancer, 
HIV/AIDS, pulmonary diseases, and critical illness (including burns and trauma). It will 
also delve into specialized nutrition support, such as enteral and parenteral nutrition. 
Students will further develop their skills in applying the nutrition care process for these 
conditions. 
 
NSM701 Nutrition and Immunity 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This covers the fundamentals of the immune system, the roles of 
specific macronutrients and micronutrients in immune cell development and function, and 
the impact of nutritional status on immune responses and susceptibility to infections. It will 
also discuss the influence of diet on inflammatory processes, autoimmune diseases, 
allergies, and the gut microbiome's role in immunity.  
 
NSM702 Pediatric and Elderly Nutrition 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course part for pediatrics will cover growth and development, 
breastfeeding and infant formula, introduction of complementary foods, nutritional

failure to thrive, childhood obesity, food allergies). The elderly nutrition section will address 
physiological changes of aging affecting nutritional status, specific nutrient needs of older 
adults, common geriatric nutritional problems (e.g., malnutrition, sarcopenia, osteoporosis), 
and nutrition in age-related chronic diseases.  
 
NSM703 Weight Management 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the etiology of weight imbalances, including 
genetic, metabolic, behavioral, and environmental factors. It explores methods for assessing 
body composition and energy expenditure, and reviews evidence-based approaches for 
weight loss, weight gain, and weight maintenance, including dietary interventions, physical 
activity, behavioral modification, pharmacological treatments, and surgical options. The 
course also addresses the prevention of weight-related health problems.  
 
NSM704 Food Processing and Preparation 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers various food processing methods, food 
preservation techniques, and principles of food packaging. It also explores the chemical and 
physical changes that occur in food during processing and cooking, and their effects on 
nutrient content and bioavailability. 
 
NSM705 Bioenergetics 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the laws of thermodynamics, the generation of 
ATP through metabolic pathways (glycolysis, citric acid cycle, oxidative phosphorylation), 
energy expenditure components (basal metabolic rate, thermic effect of food, physical 
activity), and methods for measuring energy expenditure. It will explore the regulation of 
energy balance and the metabolic adaptations to varying energy intake and expenditure 
levels.

NSM801 Metabolic Disorders and Prevention 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focus on conditions such as insulin resistance, metabolic 
syndrome, type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, gout, and inborn errors of metabolism. The course 
will explore the underlying biochemical and genetic mechanisms, diagnostic criteria, and 
the role of lifestyle factors, particularly diet and physical activity, in the development and 
progression of these disorders. Emphasis will be placed on evidence-based nutritional 
strategies for prevention and management.  
 
NSM802 Nutrition for Critically Ill 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the metabolic response to stress, trauma, sepsis, 
burns, and major surgery. It details methods for assessing nutritional requirements in 
critically ill patients, indications and contraindications for enteral and parenteral nutrition, 
selection of appropriate formulas, and monitoring for efficacy and complications of 
nutrition support. The course will also address specific nutrient requirements and adjunctive 
therapies in critical illness.  
 
NSM803 Practical Nutrition 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This capstone course aims to integrate and apply the knowledge and 
skills acquired throughout the program in practical, real-world scenarios relevant to 
nutrition and dietetics. The course review advanced topics or emerging areas in nutrition. A 
significant portion of the course will be dedicated to practical application through extended 
case studies, simulations, role-playing (e.g., counseling sessions), community project 
development, or supervised experiences in a clinical or community setting (if applicable). 
Students will practice problem-solving, critical thinking, and communication skills. The 
specific content and activities may vary to reflect current best practices and prepare students

NSM804 Research Project 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 0, Lab/TUT: 6) 
• 
Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides students with the opportunity to conduct an 
independent research project in an area of nutrition or food safety under the guidance of a 
faculty supervisor. Students will apply principles of research methodology, including 
literature review, formulating a research question/hypothesis, designing a study, data 
collection, data analysis, and interpretation. The course emphasizes critical thinking, 
scientific writing, and ethical conduct in research. Students will be required to prepare a 
comprehensive research proposal, execute the project, write a final research report or thesis, 
and present their findings orally. 
 
Elective Requirements for Technology of Nutrition and Food Safety (NSE) 
 
NSE01 Institutional Food Management 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course topic includes large-scale menu planning considering 
specific nutritional needs and budgets, procurement strategies, inventory management, food 
production systems for volume feeding, quality control, and sanitation and safety standards 
specific to institutional environments. The course also covers aspects of staff management, 
equipment utilization, and financial accountability in institutional food services. Emphasis 
is placed on delivering nutritious, safe, and cost-effective meals to diverse populations in 
institutional contexts.

NSE02 Nutrient Drug Interaction - Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) 
• 
Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course lectures cover how medications can affect nutrient 
ingestion, absorption, metabolism, and excretion, potentially leading to nutritional 
deficiencies or imbalances. Conversely, it examines how certain foods, nutrients, and 
dietary supplements can alter the pharmacokinetics (absorption, distribution, metabolism, 
excretion) and pharmacodynamics (effects) of drugs, impacting their efficacy and toxicity. 
The course will address common nutrient-drug interactions, high-risk patient populations, 
and the role of nutrition professionals in identifying, preventing, and managing these 
interactions to optimize patient outcomes and medication safety. 
 
NSE03 Phytochemicals & Nutraceuticals - Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) 
• 
Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides an overview of phytochemicals (bioactive non-
nutrient plant compounds) and nutraceuticals (foods or food components with health 
benefits beyond basic nutrition). Theoretical content includes the classification of major 
phytochemical groups (e.g., polyphenols, carotenoids, alkaloids), their sources in plant-
based foods, and their potential mechanisms of action in promoting health and preventing 
chronic diseases. The course will also discuss the concept of functional foods, the scientific 
evidence supporting the health claims of various nutraceuticals, regulatory aspects, and 
safety considerations. Students will learn to critically evaluate the role of phytochemicals 
and nutraceuticals in diet and health. 
 
NSE04 Nutritional Counseling and Communication 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on developing the essential counseling and 
communication skills required for effective nutrition practice. Theoretical lectures cover 
various counseling theories and models, techniques for behavior change, client-centered 
interviewing, active listening, and motivational interviewing. It addresses strategies for 
effective verbal and non-verbal communication, cultural sensitivity in counseling,

NSE05 Food Psychology and Sociology 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course explores the psychological and sociological factors 
influencing food choices, eating behaviors, and nutritional status. The psychological 
component examines topics such as the role of cognition, emotion, learning, and perception 
in food preferences and eating habits, as well as an introduction to eating disorders. The 
sociological component investigates how cultural norms, socioeconomic status, social 
networks, media, and public policy shape dietary patterns and food systems within 
communities and societies. 
 
NSE06 Public Nutritional Education 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, Lab/TUT: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the principles and practices of designing, 
implementing, and evaluating public nutritional education programs and campaigns. 
Theoretical aspects include understanding target audiences, developing clear and effective 
nutrition messages, selecting appropriate educational materials and communication 
channels (e.g., print, digital media, workshops), and strategies for community engagement. 
The course covers theories of health education and behavior change relevant to public 
nutrition. Students will learn to plan culturally sensitive and evidence-based nutrition 
education initiatives aimed at improving the dietary practices and health outcomes of 
diverse population groups.

Course Description of Technology of Dental Prothesis Manufacture Program 
(DP) 
 
Mandatory Requirements for Technology of Dental Prothesis Manufacture 
Technology (DPM) 
 
DPM301 Introduction to Prosthetic Dental Technology 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This introductory course provides students with an overview of the 
field of prosthetic dental technology. The course covers the history and evolution of dental 
prosthetics, the scope of practice for dental technologists, and the role of the dental 
laboratory team in oral healthcare. It introduces fundamental concepts of oral anatomy 
relevant to prosthetic design, basic dental terminology, and an overview of different types 
of dental prostheses. Ethical considerations, laboratory safety protocols, and infection 
control principles specific to dental laboratories are emphasized. 
 
DPM302 Dental Morphology and Anatomy I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course lectures focus on the identification, nomenclature, 
development, and anatomical characteristics of each individual tooth in the primary and 
permanent dentitions, including crown and root morphology, pulp cavity anatomy, and 
supporting structures. Emphasis is placed on understanding the form and function of teeth 
and their interrelationships within the dental arches. 
 
DPM303 Dental Biomaterials 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the classification, composition, physical, 
mechanical, and biological properties of various dental biomaterials, including gypsum 
products, waxes, polymers (acrylics, composites), dental ceramics, and metal alloys. The 
course emphasizes the importance of material selection based on clinical requirements, 


--- page 123 ---
 تكنولوجيا العلوم كلية
الصحية التطبيقية 
 جامعة الريادة للعلوم
والتكنولوجي
ا 
 
EL Sadat City El Mehwer El Markazy 2 
Cairo Alexandria Desert RD K92 
info@rst.edu.eg

 مدينة السادات-
 
 المحور المركزي2
 
 طريق القاهرة
 االسكندرية الصحراوي الكيلو92
 
 
Page122 
biocompatibility, and esthetic considerations. It also addresses standards and testing of 
dental materials. 
 
DPM401 Biomechanics 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 1, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers concepts such as forces, stress, strain, material 
properties (e.g., elasticity, strength, fatigue), and their relevance to the behavior of dental 
restorations under occlusal loads. It explores the biomechanical principles underlying 
different types of prostheses, including complete dentures, removable partial dentures, fixed 
partial dentures, and implant-supported restorations, focusing on factors affecting stability, 
retention, and support. 
 
DPM402 Dental Morphology and Anatomy II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM302 Dental Morphology 
and Anatomy I 
• 
Course Description: This course delves deeper into the functional aspects of tooth 
morphology, including cusp-fossa relationships, embrasures, contact areas, and contours of 
individual teeth and their arrangement in the dental arches. It will cover the anatomy of the 
temporomandibular joint (TMJ) and the muscles of mastication, and their influence on 
occlusal harmony. The relationship between tooth morphology and the design of various 
dental prostheses will be emphasized. 
 
DPM403 Principles of Occlusion 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course cover concepts of static and dynamic occlusion, 
mandibular movements, determinants of occlusion, and various occlusal schemes (e.g., 
balanced occlusion, canine guidance, group function). It explores the use of dental 
articulators, facebows, and interocclusal records in simulating jaw relationships and 
movements in the laboratory. The course also addresses occlusal considerations in different 
types of restorations and the implications of malocclusion.

Page123 
 
DPM404 Complete Denture Fabrication I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This covers patient assessment, impression making techniques and 
materials for edentulous arches, construction of custom trays, fabrication of record bases 
and occlusion rims, recording maxillomandibular relations (including centric relation and 
vertical dimension), and the selection of artificial teeth. Principles of esthetics and phonetics 
in complete denture construction will be introduced.  
 
DPM501 Fixed Prosthodontics I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles and laboratory techniques 
involved in the fabrication of fixed dental prostheses, such as crowns and bridges. The 
course covers indications and contraindications for fixed restorations, principles of tooth 
preparation design, impression materials and techniques, die preparation, and an 
introduction to various materials used for fixed prosthodontics. It also discusses concepts 
of pontic design, retainer selection, and connectors. 
 
DPM502 Complete Denture Fabrication II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM404 Complete Denture 
Fabrication I 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the advanced laboratory procedures for 
completing and finishing complete dentures. The course focus on the processing of dentures 
(flasking, packing, curing acrylic resin), finishing and polishing techniques, methods for 
denture repair, relining, and rebasing. It will also discuss immediate dentures, overdentures, 
and considerations for achieving optimal esthetics, phonetics, and function in the final 
prosthesis.

DPM503 Removable Partial Denture Fabrication I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles and techniques for designing 
and fabricating cast metallic removable partial dentures (RPDs). The course covers the 
classification of partially edentulous arches, components of an RPD (major connectors, 
minor connectors, direct retainers, indirect retainers, rests, denture bases), principles of RPD 
design, surveying and path of insertion, and an introduction to materials used for RPD 
frameworks.  
 
DPM504 Digital Dental Prosthetics I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the overview of digital workflow in dentistry, 
types of intraoral and laboratory scanners, CAD software functionalities for designing 
various dental restorations, and an introduction to different materials suitable for digital 
fabrication. File formats and basic principles of Computer-Aided Manufacturing (CAM) 
will also be discussed. 
 
DPM601 Infection Control in Dental Laboratories 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the principles of microbiology relevant to cross-
contamination, routes of transmission of infectious diseases in the dental setting, standard 
precautions, and specific infection control protocols for handling incoming dental 
impressions, casts, and appliances. It will address proper disinfection procedures for various 
dental materials and prostheses, management of contaminated laboratory items, personal 
protective equipment (PPE) for dental technologists, sterilization methods for laboratory 
instruments, and hazardous waste management.

DPM602 Digital Dental Prosthetics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 1, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM504 Digital Dental 
Prosthetics I 
• 
Course Description: This course explores advanced design features in CAD software for 
complex restorations such as multi-unit bridges, implant abutments, and partial denture 
frameworks. It will also cover different CAM technologies in more detail, including their 
capabilities, limitations, and material compatibility. Quality control in digital workflows 
and troubleshooting common issues in CAD/CAM fabrication will be addressed. 
 
DPM603 Orthodontic Appliance Fabrication I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course cover basic orthodontic terminology, classifications of 
malocclusion, the purpose and function of various removable appliances, and the properties 
of materials used in their construction. Principles of wire bending, clasp design for retention, 
and acrylic processing for orthodontic appliances will be discussed. 
 
DPM604 Fixed Prosthodontics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM501 Fixed Prosthodontics I 
• 
Course Description: This course is a continuation of Fixed Prosthodontics I, covering more 
advanced techniques and materials in the fabrication of fixed restorations. The course will 
focus on the design and fabrication of multi-unit bridges, including pontic design 
considerations and stress distribution. It will delve into metal-ceramic restorations 
(porcelain-fused-to-metal), including metal framework design, porcelain application 
techniques, and achieving esthetic outcomes. An introduction to all-ceramic restorations 
and current adhesive cementation concepts from a laboratory perspective will also be 
included.

DPM605 Removable Partial Denture Fabrication II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM503 Removable Partial 
Denture Fabrication I 
• 
Course Description: This course builds on Removable Partial Denture Fabrication I, 
focusing on the casting, finishing, and completion of cast metallic RPDs, as well as an 
introduction to other types of partial dentures. The course cover investment materials and 
techniques for RPD frameworks, casting procedures for dental alloys, finishing and 
polishing cast frameworks, and principles of attaching denture bases and artificial teeth to 
the framework. It may also introduce concepts of flexible partial dentures and other non-
metallic RPD options. 
 
DPM606 Maxillofacial Prosthetics I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This introductory course provides an overview of the field of 
maxillofacial prosthetics, which involves the prosthetic rehabilitation of patients with 
defects or disabilities of the head and neck region resulting from trauma, surgery, or 
congenital conditions. The course will cover the anatomy of the head and neck relevant to 
maxillofacial defects, classification of defects, types of intraoral and extraoral maxillofacial 
prostheses, and an introduction to the specialized materials and techniques used. The 
psychosocial aspects of patient care and the role of the maxillofacial technologist in a 
multidisciplinary team will be discussed. 
 
DPM701 Fixed Prosthodontics III 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM604 Fixed Prosthodontics II 
• 
Course Description: This advanced course in fixed prosthodontics focuses on complex 
restorative cases and specialized techniques. The course cover comprehensive treatment 
planning for extensive fixed restorations, principles of smile design, management of 
challenging occlusal schemes, and the fabrication of restorations for worn dentition. It will 
explore advanced dental materials such as newer ceramics and high-performance polymers, 
and specialized fixed prostheses like resin-bonded bridges and telescopic crowns.

DPM702 Implant-Supported Prosthodontics I 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course explores the mechanisms of speech production and the 
classification of speech sounds, emphasizing the roles of key oral structures—teeth, tongue, 
palate, and lips—in articulation. It investigates the influence of dental prostheses, 
particularly complete and partial dentures, on speech patterns and clarity. The course further 
examines common phonetic issues linked to prosthetic use and highlights how specific 
design elements, such as tooth positioning, palatal contour, and denture base thickness, can 
be modified to improve speech intelligibility and maximize patient comfort.  
 
DPM703 Troubleshooting in Prosthetic Dental Technology 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on identifying, analyzing, and resolving common 
technical problems and complications encountered during the fabrication of various dental 
prostheses. The course cover common errors in complete dentures, removable partial 
dentures, fixed prosthodontics, and implant restorations, discussing their causes, 
consequences, and corrective measures. Topics include issues related to impressions, cast 
pouring, material handling, processing errors, occlusal discrepancies, esthetic failures, and 
misfit of prostheses. 
 
DPM704 Orthodontic Appliance Fabrication II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM603 Orthodontic Appliance 
Fabrication I 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces fixed orthodontic components that dental 
technologists may be involved with, functional appliances, and an introduction to clear 
aligner technology from a fabrication perspective. It will also cover techniques for 
incorporating specialized elements like expansion screws and auxiliary springs. The 
tutorial/laboratory sessions will focus on the design and fabrication of these advanced

DPM705 Quality Assurance in Dental Prosthetics 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the importance of quality in dental prosthetics, 
establishing quality standards, developing standard operating procedures (SOPs), 
documentation and record-keeping, material batch traceability, and equipment calibration 
and maintenance. It will also discuss methods for identifying and minimizing errors at 
various stages of prosthesis fabrication, implementing corrective and preventive actions 
(CAPA), and adhering to regulatory requirements and best practice guidelines. The course 
aims to instill a commitment to continuous quality improvement in the provision of dental 
prosthetic services. 
 
DPM801 Maxillofacial Prosthetics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM606 Maxillofacial 
Prosthetics I 
• 
Course Description: This advanced course cover advanced impression techniques for large 
defects, digital technology in maxillofacial prosthetics (scanning, CAD/CAM), 
osseointegrated implants for craniofacial retention, and advanced material science for facial 
silicones and acrylics, including intrinsic and extrinsic coloration techniques. The course 
will also address challenges in fabricating speech aids, radiation shields, and other 
specialized appliances. 
 
DPM802 Implant-Supported Prosthodontics II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: DPM702 Implant Prosthodontics 
I 
• 
Course Description: This course cover advanced treatment planning, biomechanics of 
implant-supported full-arch prostheses (e.g., hybrid dentures, overdentures retained by bars 
or individual attachments), guided surgery concepts from a laboratory perspective, and

DPM803 Dental Radiology Technique 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This covers the fundamentals of radiation physics, radiation biology 
and safety, types of dental radiographic images (intraoral and extraoral), and common 
radiographic techniques. While dental technologists do not typically take radiographs, this 
course will focus on the interpretation of radiographs to understand dental anatomy, identify 
pathological conditions, assess bone quality for implants, and evaluate the fit and placement 
of prosthetic restorations. 
 
DPM804 Graduation Research in Dental Technology 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 0, TUT/LAB: 6) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This capstone course allows students to undertake an independent 
research project or an in-depth literature review on a topic relevant to prosthetic dental 
technology. Under the guidance of a faculty supervisor, students will learn to formulate a 
research question or topic, conduct a comprehensive literature search, critically appraise 
scientific literature, and develop a research proposal. The course emphasizes scientific 
writing, critical thinking, problem-solving, and ethical considerations in research. Students 
will be required to produce a substantial written report or dissertation and may be required 
to present their findings. This project aims to integrate knowledge gained throughout the 
program and foster an evidence-based approach to dental technology. 
 
Elective Requirements for Technology of Dental Prosthesis Manufacture  
 
DPE01 Laboratories Management 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course cover topics such as laboratory design and workflow 
optimization, financial management (budgeting, cost control, pricing), inventory

DPE02 Phonetics 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course covers the mechanisms of speech production, the 
classification of speech sounds, and the functional roles of oral structures—such as the teeth, 
tongue, palate, and lips—in articulation. It examines the impact of various dental prostheses, 
particularly complete and partial dentures, on speech performance. The course also 
addresses common phonetic challenges associated with prosthetic use and discusses how 
specific design factors, including tooth positioning, palatal contour, and denture base 
thickness—can be adjusted to enhance speech clarity and overall patient comfort.  
 
DPE03 Color & Appearance 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course delves into the science and art of color and appearance in 
dental prosthetics, crucial for achieving esthetically pleasing restorations. Theoretical 
lectures and tutorials will cover the fundamentals of color theory (hue, value, chroma), light 
and optics, metamerism, perception of color, and factors influencing color matching in 
dentistry. It will discuss various shade selection systems and instruments, communication 
of shade information between the clinic and laboratory, and techniques for characterizing 
and customizing restorations (e.g., staining, layering ceramics) to mimic natural tooth 
appearance. The course will also address the impact of different dental materials on the final 
esthetic outcome. 
 
DPE004 Dental Photography 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None

DPE04 Principle of Esthetic Dentistry 1 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course cover concepts of smile design, facial analysis, dental 
anatomical harmony, and the role of tooth morphology, color, texture, and position in 
creating esthetic outcomes. It will introduce different esthetic materials (e.g., ceramics, 
composites) and their indications. The course will also discuss communication with the 
clinician regarding esthetic expectations and treatment planning.  
 
DPE05 Lasers in Dentistry 
• 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course provides an overview of the application of lasers in 
various fields of dentistry, with a focus on aspects relevant to dental laboratory technology. 
Theoretical lectures and tutorials will cover basic laser physics, types of lasers used in 
dentistry, laser-tissue interaction, and safety protocols. While direct clinical use is by 
dentists, this course will explore how lasers are used in procedures that interface with 
laboratory work, such as soft tissue contouring around restorations, implant uncovering, or 
even potentially in laboratory procedures like welding or surface treatment of materials. 
 
DPE06 Principle of Esthetic Dentistry 2 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None  
• 
Course Description: This course cover advanced smile design principles, managing 
complex esthetic challenges, advanced applications of ceramic materials (e.g., laminate 
veneers, advanced layering techniques), and integrating digital technology into esthetic 
workflows. The course will emphasize the importance of meticulous technique and artistic 
skill in replicating natural dental esthetics.

Requirements for Technology of Anesthesia and Critical Care Program 
 
Mandatory Requirements for Technology of Anesthesia and Critical Care 
Program (ACM) 
 
ACM301 Infection Control and Occupational Safety 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course covers the principles and practices of infection control and 
occupational safety in healthcare settings, with a focus on anesthesia and critical care environments. 
Topics include standard precautions, sterilization, disinfection, waste management, and safety 
protocols for healthcare professionals. Practical sessions involve applying these principles in 
simulated scenarios.
 
ACM302 Intensive Care Technology-I 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course is designed to enable students to understand types and design of 
intensive care units (ICU) and their objectives, basic equipment, supplies and medications in ICU, 
and to assist students in understanding the principles of medical ethics, communication. 
 
ACM303 First aid and emergency 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 1, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course provides students with essential knowledge and skills in first aid 
and emergency medical response. Topics include patient assessment, CPR, management of 
common medical emergencies, and basic trauma care. Practical sessions involve hands-on practice 
of first aid and emergency procedures. 
 
ACM401 Critical Care Nutrition 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course focuses on the nutritional needs of critically ill patients and the 
methods of nutritional support in the intensive care unit. Topics include metabolic changes in

Page138 
critical illness, enteral and parenteral nutrition, and nutritional assessment. Practical sessions 
involve case study analysis and nutritional support calculations. 
 
ACM402 Intensive Care Equipment I 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles of operation, maintenance, and 
troubleshooting of essential equipment used in the intensive care unit. Topics include ventilators, 
infusion pumps, cardiac monitors, and oxygen delivery systems. Practical sessions involve hands-
on experience with these devices. 
 
ACM403 Mechanical ventilation 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course covers the principles and techniques of mechanical ventilation. 
Topics include respiratory physiology, modes of ventilation, ventilator settings, patient monitoring 
during mechanical ventilation, and weaning strategies. Practical sessions involve ventilator 
operation and simulated patient management. 
 
ACM404 Pharmacology for Respiratory Technologists 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course introduces the basic principles of pharmacology with a focus on 
medications used in respiratory care and critical care. Topics include drug classification, 
pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and common medications used in the ICU and anesthesia. 
 
ACM501 Patient monitoring and instrument in ICU I  
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: International system for units, Conversion from one system to another, 
Measurement and monitoring pulse, blood pressure, CVP, Pulmonary artery pressure, temperature, 
ventilation, blood gases arterial PH, ECG and ECG interpretation, Monitoring neuromuscular 
function, Calculation of blood loss during surgery.

ACM502 Transfusion practice in critical care 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: Study the different technique for blood transfusion and ways to keep blood 
fresh and away from any damage and transfusion of different fluid. 
 
ACM503 Anesthesia Equipment 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This will prepare the student for the complete understanding and usage of the 
equipment used in the practice of anesthesia including the anesthesia workstation, breathing 
systems, monitors, advanced airway devices, ventilators, infusion equipment and defibrillator. 
 
ACM504 Intensive Care Technology II 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: ACM302 
Course Description: This course gives the students the needed knowledge to identify the necessary 
procedures for patients exhibiting various health problems and practicing it. 
 
ACM601 Intensive care equipment II 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: ACM402 
Course Description: Tracheal tube and associated equipment, Lung isolation device, Device for 
managing the difficult airway, Gas monitoring, Airway volumes, flows and pressure, Temperature 
control equipment, Equipment checkout and maintenance. Cleaning and sterilization of equipment. 
 
ACM602 Maintenance of medical equipment  
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course provides the students with the principles of different equipment 
and medical devices, those related to their specialties. Students will gain basic knowledge about 
how these devices are working, how to monitor, detect and report any malfunction.
 
 
ACM603 Epidemiology 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 


--- page 141 ---
