Course Description: The course is designed to help the student to acquire knowledge related to 
the basic principles, methods, uses of epidemiology and its role in public health. As well as the 
factors associated with the etiology and distribution of health and disease, describing disease 
frequency in populations, appropriate summaries and displays of data. In addition, students will 
develop skills to read, interpret, and evaluate health information from published epidemiologic 
studies. 
 
ACM604 Basic nutrition and metabolism 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course aims to give the students good information about the major 
components of food, namely carbohydrates, proteins, lipids, vitamins, minerals, and water with 
emphasis on daily requirements for normal subject and nutritional restrictions on certain diseases. 
Medical problems related to nutrition such as obesity, cachexia and nutritional deficiency diseases 
are to be emphasized. It also includes an overview. 
of the nutritional consequences and metabolic challenges posed by acute and chronic diseases. 
 
ACM605 Basics of Anesthesia Technology I 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course provides students with basics that are essential for the work in 
the field of anesthesia technology. It enables students understand, the medical gas supplies and 
piped services, airway management and oxygen therapy. 
 
ACM606 Principle of airway management 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 3, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: Basic airway management, Advance airway management, Orotracheal 
intubation indications and technique, Nasotracheal intubation indications and technique, Perform 
sellick maneuver, Complications of intubation procedures. 
 
ACM701 Patient Safety 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None

Course Description: Science of safety, errors and adverse events in health care, models of safety 
and change, culture of safety, detection and reporting of injuries and errors, investigative methods, 
disclosure of adverse events, improvements of clinical systems, policy intervention  
 
ACM702 Advanced Intensive Care Technology I 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course gives the students advanced concepts in advanced life support, 
advanced skills in care of the unconscious patient, basic administration. 
 
ACM703 Applied Intensive Care Technology I 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course gives the students the needed skills in respiratory support 
including all procedures for therapy and monitoring of respiratory disorders. 
 
ACM704 Patient monitoring and instrument in ICU II  
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: ACM501 
Course Description: Interpret the capnogram, Medical gases, Monitor continuous cardiac output 
Monitor the control breathing, Peripheral neuromuscular function monitoring Arterial blood gas 
monitoring, Acid base assessment, Pulse oximetry, Transcutaneous oxygen monitoring , 
Monitoring of mixed venous oxygenation, Gut mucosal PH monitoring, Transcutaneous monitoring 
of co2 tension and control of breathing, Non -invasive monitoring of ventilation , monitoring the 
use of tracheal tubes, Pulmonary artery catheterization interpretation and pressure recording, 
hemodynamic profile interpretation, Continuous cardiac assist devices, bispectral index, 
Monitoring and interpreting intracranial pressure, Peripheral neuromuscular function monitoring, 
patient monitoring during transportation, standard monitoring. 
 
ACM801 Basics of Anesthesia Technology II 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: ACM605

Course Description: This course provides students with basics that are essential for the work in 
the field of anesthesia technology. It enables students to understand humidification, suction, 
scavenging and control of pollution, alarm devices and devices used for regional anesthesia. 
 
ACM802 Advanced Intensive Care Technology II 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: ACM702 
Course Description: This course is designed to enable students to understand the basics of airway 
care, oxygen therapy and the principle of assessing and managing patients in ICU including 
developing a plan of care. 
 
ACM803 Problem solving and decision making in critical care. 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: Premedication of adults, premedication of children, tracheal intubation, 
difficult airway unrecognized cannot ventilate, cannot intubate. Monitoring in hypoxemia, 
decreased inspired oxygen concentration, increase peak airway pressure, response to low pressure 
alarm, the full stomach patient, elderly patient, renal insufficiency, diabetes mellitus, obesity, 
asthma, wheezing, chronic obstructive 101 pulmonary disease. Know and suspect tuberculosis, 
pulmonary hypertension, restrictive lung disease, cardiac artery disease. Prevention and control 
principles with emphasis on indwelling device care, hand hygiene and multi-drug-resistant 
organisms among heath care personnel. Common complication in intensive care e.g., fever and 
urinary tract infection. 
 
ACM804 Pulmonary diseases 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: The purpose of this course is for students to gain knowledge and to 
understand the incidence, etiology, clinical manifestations, pathophysiology, and differential 
diagnosis of pathologies in acute lung diseases. While studying each individual disease, the 
evidence-based treatment and prevention strategies, including pharmacology, will be examined.

ACM805 Graduation Project  
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 0, TUT/LAB: 6) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: In this course, students will complete their project work, data analysis, 
presenting data through writing and at the end of the semester, the students' teams are required to 
submit written end of semester reports and participate in departmental oral presentations for 
examination and discuss their projects results and their presentation. Projects will be evaluated by 
graduation project committee that assigned by department council, according to oral presentation 
and discussion of the dissertation. 
 
Elective Requirements for Technology of Anesthesia and Critical Care 
Program (ACE) 
 
ACE001: Healthcare Technology Management 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course focuses on the strategic and operational aspects of 
managing medical technology within healthcare organizations. The course covers topics 
such as technology assessment, acquisition planning, equipment inventory management, 
maintenance strategies (corrective, preventive, predictive), service contracts, staff training, 
asset tracking, and financial management related to healthcare technology. The role of 
technology in improving healthcare delivery and patient safety is emphasized. 
 
ACE002: Healthcare Quality Systems and Standards 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• 
Course Description: This course introduces the principles of quality management systems 
and relevant standards in the healthcare technology sector. The course covers concepts of 
quality assurance, quality control, continuous quality improvement (CQI) methodologies, 
ISO standards (e.g., ISO 9001, ISO 13485 for medical devices), accreditation processes, 
risk management, and regulatory requirements related to the quality of medical equipment 
and healthcare services.

 طريق القاهرة
 االسكندرية الصحراوي الكيلو92
 
 
Page144 
ACE003 Laboratory Investigations in Intensive Care Units (ICU) 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course focuses on the critical role of laboratory investigations in the 
management of patients in Intensive Care Units (ICUs). The theoretical component covers the 
selection, interpretation, and clinical utility of various urgent laboratory tests used for monitoring 
critically ill patients, including blood gases, electrolytes, cardiac markers, coagulation profiles, 
sepsis markers, and organ function tests. The course will emphasize rapid turnaround times, point-
of-care testing in the ICU setting, and the integration of laboratory data into critical care decision-
making. 
 
ACE004 Health Communication and Patient Education 
Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course aims to equip medical laboratory students with effective 
communication skills essential for interacting with patients, healthcare professionals, and the 
public, as well as principles of patient education relevant to laboratory testing. The theoretical 
component covers verbal and non-verbal communication, active listening, empathy, cultural 
sensitivity, explaining complex laboratory information in simple terms, and addressing patient 
concerns about tests and results. Strategies for developing patient education materials and 
promoting health literacy related to laboratory services will also be explored. 
 
 
ACE005 Bio-Medical Waste Management 
Credit Hours: 2 (2 LEC) - Prerequisites: None 
Course Description: This course addresses the critical aspects of managing bio-medical waste 
generated in healthcare and laboratory settings. The course covers the classification of biomedical 
waste, potential hazards associated with different types of waste, and principles of waste 
segregation, collection, transportation, treatment, and disposal according to national and 
international regulations. It emphasizes infection control, safety protocols for healthcare workers, 
and environmental protection. Strategies for waste minimization and recycling will also be 
discussed.

Course Description of Technology of Optics Program 
 
Mandatory Requirements for Technology of Technology of Optics 
Program (OPM) - 74 Credit Hours 
 
OPM301 Introduction to Optics 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course introduces the fundamental principles of optics, including 
the nature of light, reflection, refraction, interference, diffraction, and polarization. Basic 
concepts of geometrical and physical optics are covered. Practical sessions involve 
experiments demonstrating these principles. 
 
OPM302 Ocular Anatomy and Physiology 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course provides a detailed study of the anatomy and physiology 
of the human eye and its adnexa. Topics include the structure of the cornea, lens, retina, and 
visual pathway, as well as the physiological processes involved in vision. Practical sessions 
involve anatomical model studies and physiological demonstrations. 
 
OPM303 Geometrical, Physical and Visual optics  
• Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This is an introductory course in geometrical optics. Introduction to 
light and optics, wavelength, frequency, speed of light, index of refraction, and optical 
length will be discussed. Image formation by plane mirrors and their properties, Image 
formation by spherical convex mirrors, concave mirrors, properties of the image and 
magnification, Refraction through plane surfaces, image deviation, real and apparent depth, 
Prisms, ophthalmic prisms, image deviation, minimum deviation, Introduction curvature 
and spherical surfaces, sag formula, calculation of power, radius of curvature, Refraction 
through spherical surfaces, convex surfaces, concave surfaces, image formation, 
magnification, focal length, surface power. Thin lenses, types, power, focal lengths, surface

power, sphero-cylindrical lenses, focal planes, power 
 
OPM401 Environmental optometry  
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course is designed to give an idea about the relation between 
vision and environment including home, work, recreation, and transportation. Protecting the 
eye from injury, maximizing visual performance, industrial ophthalmic performance and 
requirement for motor vehicle and machinery licens. 
 
OPM402 Visual Science 1 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course covers the fundamental principles of visual perception, 
including psychophysics, color vision, spatial vision, and temporal vision. Practical sessions 
involve experiments exploring various aspects of visual perception. 
 
OPM403 Biomaterials in ophthalmology  
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course introduces the types, properties, and biocompatibility of 
materials used in ophthalmic devices, including contact lenses, intraocular lenses, and 
spectacle lenses. 
 
 
OPM404 Optometric instruments 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course provides the basic technology of ophthalmic instruments 
like Slit lamp principles, Goldman Applanation tonometer calibration, Shoitz calibration, 
Tonopen, 
Airpuff 
tonometer, 
Auto 
refractometer, 
Optical 
Keratometer 
and

principles, Phaco machines principles, Vitrectomy machine principles will also be covered. 
 
OPM501 Ocular microbiology for optometrists 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course is designed to provide students with a comprehensive 
understanding of microorganisms and their role in ocular diseases. The course covers 
etiology, epidemiology, pathogenesis, diagnosis, and management of immune-mediated 
inflammatory and microbial ocular diseases. It focuses on the practical application of 
microbiology in clinical optometry. Ocular microbiology and infection control for 
optometrists: Causes of the most common infectious agents affecting eye,  
 
OPM502 Contact lenses I 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: The course aims to provide knowledge of different contact lens types, 
indications, and contraindications for wearing contact lenses, fitting contact lenses and 
different contact lens accessories. The history of contact lens development is discussed 
leading to current lens materials, soft lens design and manufacturing techniques. Parameters 
such as DK, DK/l, wetting angle, water content, thickness, edge, diameter, monocurve 
versus multicurve design and sagittal depth will be discussed. Fitting considerations of soft 
lenses for optimal physiological function; sag, movement on blink; evaluation of VA, OR, 
OK, slit-lamp examination and vertex calculations. Criteria for patient selection and training 
as well as discussion of contact lens care systems. 
 
OPM503 Ophthalmic Optics I 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: Typically cover the fundamental principles of spectacle lens design, 
function, and clinical application, including lens materials, shapes, and aberrations. They 
also delve into frame materials, types, and specifications, as well as practical skills like 
mounting lenses, fitting frames, and understanding lens power. Additionally, the course

OPM504 Laser in ophthalmology  
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course explains the ophthalmic lasers currently commercially 
available, including the technology used during the different procedures. The course helps 
the students to understand how these devices are working, how to identify any malfunction 
and when to expect complications. This course also provides the basic principles of 
recognition, evaluation, and control of potential hazards. It will review laser operations, the 
associated potential hazards, responsibilities of the laser user community, and the services 
provided by the radiation safety program to help in the safe use of laser radiation. 
 
OPM601 Properties of fiber Optics 
• Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: A course on the properties of fiber optics would typically cover the 
fundamental principles of optical fibers and their applications in communication systems. 
It would delve into the construction, characteristics, and performance of different types of 
optical fibers, along with the components and technologies used in fiber optic systems. The 
course might also explore the physics of light, including reflection, refraction, and 
absorption, as they relate to optical fibers. 
 
OPM602 Errors of refractions 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: In this course, a review of optical properties of the eye, schematic and 
reduced eyes, optics of the cycloplegic eye, measurement of parameters of the eye, 
accommodation, refractive errors, myopia, hypermetropia, astigmatism, visual axes, 
anisometropia, emmetropia, presbyopia, trial lens set, prisms, IPD, Phoropters, pinhole, 
Maddox rod , stenopic slit.

 مدينة السادات-

 طريق القاهرة
 االسكندرية الصحراوي الكيلو92
 
 
Page154 
OPM603 Infection Control and Occupational Safety 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: An "Infection Control and Occupational Safety" course aims to 
educate participants on how to prevent the spread of infections in various settings, including 
healthcare and workplaces. It covers topics like the chain of infection, hygiene practices, 
proper use of personal protective equipment (PPE), and measures to minimize infection 
risks. The course also emphasizes the importance of creating and maintaining effective 
infection prevention. 
 
OPM604 Technology of spectacles 
• Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course will cover ophthalmic materials of spectacle lenses, 
optical and physical characteristics of ophthalmic lenses, prisms and decentration, spherical 
and aspheric lenses, multifocal lenses, lens power measurement methods, lens power-
thickness relationships and aberration theory and its application to lens design. 
 
OPM605 Ophthalmic Optics II 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: OPM503 
• Course Description: This course build upon foundational optics knowledge, focusing on 
advanced lens design, dispensing techniques, and clinical applications. Students learn about 
multifocal, progressive, and specialized lens types, including their impact on vision and 
patient comfort. The course also covers lens fitting, adjustment, and repair, along with the 
optics of prisms and their use in vision correction. 
 
OPM606 Digital Optics Technology 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 3, TUT/ LAB:0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course focuses on the principles and applications of optics, with 
an emphasis on the integration of digital technologies to enhance optical systems. It may 
cover topics like micro-optics, diffractive optics, and optical fiber communication, along

OPM701 Visual Science II 
• 
Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: OPM402 
• Course Description: To provide an understanding of visual information processing in the 
human brain. In particular, the cortical processing of shape, motion and color, and 
development of the visual cortex will be addressed. A problem-oriented approach will 
develop critical thinking and critical thinking skills. Students will acquire the ability to seek, 
evaluate, and retrieve scientific information on which to base their clinical practice. 
 
OPM702 Contact lens II. 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: OPM502 
• Course Description: This second course will discuss topics pertaining to fitting rigid 
lenses. rigid lens design, materials, care regimens, insertion, and removal. Fitting techniques 
and lacrimal lens, fluorescein patterns and alignment, flat and steep fits as well as bearing 
area and intra-palpebral fit, over-refraction as well as lacrimal lens calculations and 
correlations. Patient selection and adaptation will also be discussed. Special lens designs 
and fitting of toric hard and soft lenses, bifocal lenses, aphakic lenses, prism ballast, 
truncation, fenestration, lens carriers, extended wear and fitting keratoconic patients will be 
discussed. 
 
OPM703 Laser Safety 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: A Laser Safety course provides fundamental training on the safe use 
of lasers and laser systems. It covers basic laser concepts, hazard evaluations, potential 
biological effects, safety practices, and relevant regulations, empowering learners to create 
a positive culture of laser safety.

• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None

and practice monitoring, fault detection and reporting any malfunction. 
 
OPM801 Ophthalmic instruments 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: An Ophthalmic Instruments course focuses on the testing, 
instrumentation, and maintenance of equipment used in eye care, equipping students with 
the skills to diagnose and manage eye conditions. The course covers topics such as ocular 
anatomy, optics, and pathology, along with practical training in operating and maintaining 
equipment like slit lamps, keratometers, and ophthalmoscopes. 
 
OPM802 Ocular imaging techniques 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course introduces the fundamental techniques of ophthalmic 
imaging, including Optical Coherence Tomography (OCT), ophthalmic photography 
(Fundus and Slit Lamp), Fluorescein Angiography (FA), and Fundus Autofluorescence 
(FAF). It also covers additional imaging modalities commonly used in ophthalmology, such 
as CT, MRI, Corneal Confocal Tomography (CCT), Heidelberg Retinal Tomography 
(HRT), ocular ultrasonography (A-scan and B-scan), optical biometry, Indocyanine Green 
Angiography (ICG), Visual Evoked Potential (VEP), Electroretinography (ERG), 
dacryocyntegraphy, Pentacam imaging, Ocular Response Analyzer (ORA), Corvis, and 
external photography. 
 
OPM803 Binocular vision  
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course introduces the student to Accommodation, binocular 
muscular coordination and anomalies, binocular vision, It provides the basic knowledge of 
extra ocular muscle anatomy and innervation, heterotropias, microtropias, amblyopia,

OPM804 Artificial Eye Technology Ocular imaging techniques 
• Credit Hours: 2 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: The technology of making artificial eyes is a staple part of the optical 
technology student's training. The course provides the basics of this technology regarding 
indications, types of devices used, and complications. 
 
OPM805 Artificial Eye Technology Ocular imaging techniques 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: The project aims to develop students' abilities in research, design, 
experimentation, critical thinking, and problem-solving. Students will select a project topic 
in consultation with a faculty supervisor, conduct a thorough literature review, and develop 
a comprehensive project proposal. The project work may involve theoretical analysis, 
experimental investigation, system design and simulation, or the development and testing 
of optical components or systems. Upon completion, students are required to submit a 
detailed written project report (thesis) and deliver a final oral presentation and defense of 
their project work and findings. 
 
Elective Requirements for Technology of Technology of Optics Program 
(OPM) – 6 Credit Hours 
 
OPE001 First aid and emergency 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: It includes cuts, scratches, objects in the eye, Burns, chemical 
exposure & lilient injuries to the eye-on-eye lid. Certain eye infections & other medical 
conditions e.g., liload clots on glaucoma.

• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None

management roles within healthcare organizations. It equips students with the knowledge 
and skills to manage healthcare facilities, improve patient care, and navigate the 
complexities of the healthcare industry. The course covers a wide range of topics, including 
strategic planning, resource allocation, quality improvement, and healthcare policy. 
 
OPE003 Digital Optics Applications 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/ LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course covers the principles and applications of using digital 
techniques to manipulate and control light, often focusing on areas like optical design, 
imaging, and fabrication. It would delve into how digital optics are used in various fields, 
such as micro-optics, nanophotonics, and optical sensing. 
 
OPE004 Optical electronics 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 1, TUT/LAB: 2) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course introduces the fundamentals of optical electronics, 
covering the principles and applications of electronic components and circuits in optical and 
photonic devices relevant to vision technology. 
 
OPE005 Health Communication and Patient Education 
• Credit Hours: 3 (LEC: 2, TUT/LAB: 0) - Prerequisites: None 
• Course Description: This course aims to equip medical laboratory students with effective 
communication skills essential for interacting with patients, healthcare professionals, and 
the public, as well as principles of patient education relevant to laboratory testing. The 
course covers verbal and non-verbal communication, active listening, empathy, cultural 
sensitivity, explaining complex laboratory information in simple terms, and addressing 
patient concerns about tests and results. Strategies for developing patient education 
materials and promoting health literacy related to laboratory services will also be explored.
